In the few days at the barracks of the 200th Infantry, Sam had learned something of regimental work, and now he applied himself assiduously to the study of the business of the headquarters of a general in command in the field, for the army was practically in the field. At first it all seemed to him to be a maze quite without a plan, and he hoped that in time he would begin to see the outline of a system. But the more he observed the less system he saw. Everything that could be postponed was postponed. Responsibility was shifted from one staff officer to another. No one was held accountable for anything, and general confusion seemed to reign. The place was besieged with contractors and agents, and the staff was nearly worried to death. The general was always very busy﻿—fanning himself﻿—and the days went on.

One morning a fellow member of the staff, a young lieutenant whom he scarcely knew, called Sam aside and asked him for a half-hour’s conference. They went off together into a deserted room, and the lieutenant began the conversation in a whisper.

===Chinese===
在第二百步兵团兵营的那几天里，萨姆对团里的事务已经略知一二，如今他又一头扎进去，刻苦钻研一位野战指挥将军司令部里的门道，因为这支军队实际上已处于战地状态。起初，这一切在他看来全然是一座没有章法的迷宫，他还指望假以时日，自己能看出一点体系的轮廓来。可是他越观察，越看不出什么体系。凡是能推迟的事一概推迟。责任从一个参谋身上推到另一个参谋身上。没有一个人对任何事情负责，处处似乎是一片大乱。承包商和代理商把此地围了个水泄不通，参谋们几乎被烦得要死。将军总是忙得不可开交——忙着扇扇子——日子就这样一天天过去。

一天早晨，参谋部里的一位同僚——一个他几乎不认识的年轻中尉——把萨姆拉到一旁，要求同他密谈半个小时。两人一起走进一间无人使用的屋子，中尉压低嗓门开了腔。

===Original===
“See here, Captain,” said he, “we’re looking for a patriotic fellow who cares more for his country than his own reputation. We understand that you’re just the man.”

“I hope so,” said Sam, delighted at the prospect of an opportunity to distinguish himself.

“It’s a rather delicate matter,” continued the lieutenant, “and I must say it’s rather a compliment to you to be selected for the job. The fact is, that Captain Jones is in trouble. He’s about $3,000 short in his accounts.”

“How did that happen?” asked Sam.

“Oh, that’s not the point. I don’t see that it makes any difference. But we’ve got to get him out of the scrape. The honor of the army is at stake. Civilians don’t understand us. They don’t appreciate our standards of honor. And if this thing gets out they’ll charge us with all kinds of things. We’ve got to raise $3,000. That’s all there is of it.”

“Good heavens! how can we?” cried Sam. “I’ve hardly got anything left of my pay, but I can give, say $25, on the next payday.”

===Chinese===
“是这样，上尉，”他说，“我们正在找一位爱国志士——一位把国家看得比自己的名誉还重的人。我们了解下来，您正是这样一位。”

“但愿如此。”萨姆说，眼见建功立业的机会临头，心中大喜。

“这事相当微妙，”中尉接着说，“而且我得说，选中您去办这件事，本身就是对您的一种抬举。事情是这样的：琼斯上尉（Captain Jones）遇上麻烦了。他的账上亏空了大约$3,000。”

“那是怎么弄出来的？”萨姆问。

“哦，那不是问题的关键。我看这也不影响什么。反正我们得把他从这摊烂泥里捞出来。陆军的荣誉危在旦夕。平民百姓不理解我们，不懂得我们军人的荣誉标准。这件事一旦捅出去，他们什么罪名都会安到我们头上。我们必须凑出$3,000。就是这么回事。”

“老天爷！咱们怎么凑得出来？”萨姆嚷道，“我的薪饷也剩不下几个钱了，不过到下个发饷日，我大概还能捐个$25。”

===Original===
“We’re not going to pass the hat around. That would be beneath the dignity of the army. What we want you to do is this﻿—and, indeed, we have settled it that you should do it. You are to go tomorrow afternoon to Banks & Company, the army contractors, and have a confidential talk with Banks. Tell him you must have $3,000 at once. Here’s a letter of introduction to him. He will see that you represent the people that run things here. Tell him that his contracts will probably be preferred to Short & Co.’s, and tell him that for the future we shan’t inspect his things as closely as we have in the past. You needn’t go into particulars. He will understand. It’s an ordinary business matter.”

“I don’t quite like the idea,” said Sam, ruminating. “Why don’t you go yourself?”

“My dear Captain, I’m only a lieutenant. It requires a man of higher rank to do such an important piece of work. You’re a new man on the staff, and we wanted to pay you an honor and give you a chance to show your patriotism. You will be saving the reputation and character of the army.”

“Oh, thank you!” exclaimed Sam. “Are you sure that it’s always done in just this way?”

“Always. It’s an ordinary matter of business arrangement, as I’ve already told you.”

“Then it must be all right, I suppose,” said Sam.

===Chinese===
“我们可不打算四处凑份子。那样做有失陆军的体面。我们要您做的是这么一桩事——而且，说实话，我们已经商定由您去办。您明天下午到陆军承包商班克斯公司（Banks & Company）去一趟，同班克斯（Banks）本人密谈一次。告诉他，您必须立刻拿到$3,000。这是给他的一封介绍信。他自然会看出，您代表着此地当家主事的人物。告诉他，他的合同多半会优先于肖特公司（Short & Co.）的；再告诉他，往后我们查验他的货品，不会像从前那样苛刻。细节您不必多谈。他心里会明白的。这不过是一桩寻常的生意往来。”

“这个主意我不大喜欢，”萨姆沉吟道，“你自己为什么不去？”

“亲爱的上尉，我不过是个中尉。这样重要的差事，得由军衔更高的人来办。您是参谋部的新人，我们正想给您一份体面，也让您有个机会显示您的爱国心。您此举拯救的，可是陆军的声誉和品格。”

“哦，谢谢您！”萨姆脱口道，“您敢保这种事向来都是这么办的吗？”

“向来如此。这不过是一桩寻常的生意安排，我方才已经说过了。”

“那想必是不会错的。”萨姆说。

===Original===
“But it’s not only that. It’s a noble act to protect the character of a brother officer.”

“So it is, so it is,” said Sam. “I’ll do it. I’ll call and see him about it tomorrow afternoon.”

“Hello!” shouted another officer, coming into the room. “Have you seen the orders? There’s to be a conference of brigade and regimental commanders here tonight, and all staff officers are invited to attend. That means business.”

Sam was overjoyed at the news, and the three men hastened to the headquarters’ room to discuss it with their fellow officers.

===Chinese===
“还不止于此。保护一位袍泽军官的品格，这是一桩高尚的行为。”

“正是，正是，”萨姆说，“我去了。明天下午我就去拜访他，把这事办了。”

“喂！”另一名军官嚷着走进屋来，“你们看到命令没有？今晚要在这里召开旅长和团长的会议，全体参谋军官均应邀出席。这回可是要动真格的了。”

萨姆听了这个消息喜出望外，三人赶紧奔到司令部那间屋里，同其他参谋军官议论开了。

===Original===
Sam was present at the conference as a matter of course, and he watched the proceedings with the greatest interest. A map was stretched out on a magnificent gilt table in the middle of the room in which Sam had first seen the general, and most of the officers bent over it studying it. The general sat back in his armchair with his fan and asked everybody’s advice, and no one appeared to have any advice to give.

“The fact is this, gentlemen,” he said at last, “we’ve got to do something, and the question is, what to do. Burton,” said he to his assistant adjutant-general, “show them the plan that we’ve worked out.”

Burton was one of the officers who were poring over the map, and he began to explain a general advance in the direction of the enemy. He pointed out the position which they were now supposed to occupy, some ten miles away.

“We ought to move out our lines tomorrow,” he explained, “within, say, three or four miles of theirs. The regiments will keep the same order that they’re in here at Havilla. We can’t make the final arrangements until we get there. We may stay there a day or two to entrench ourselves, and then move on them at daybreak some day within a week.”

===Chinese===
萨姆理所当然地出席了会议，怀着极大的兴趣旁观整个过程。一幅地图铺开在屋子中央一张华丽的镀金桌子上——正是萨姆初次见到将军的那间屋子——大多数军官都俯身在地图上研读。将军靠在扶手椅里摇着扇子，征询每个人的意见，可谁像是什么意见也提不出来。

“实情是这样的，诸位，”他终于开口了，“我们总得干点什么，问题在于干什么。伯顿（Burton），”他对副官长助理说道，“把咱们拟好的方案拿给他们看看。”

伯顿正是俯身地图研读的那些军官之一，他开始讲解一个朝敌军方向的全面推进方案。他指出敌军目前据守的阵地，大约在十英里开外。

“咱们明天就该把战线推出去，”他讲解道，“推进到距敌军阵地大约三四英里的地方。各团保持眼下在哈维拉的次序。到了那边才能做最后的部署。咱们或许在那里驻上一两天，构筑堑壕，然后在一周之内的某个拂晓向他们发起攻击。”

===Original===
“That’s the plan, gentlemen,” said the general. “What do you think of it?” and he began to question all the general and field officers present beginning with the youngest, and none of them had any suggestion to offer.

“Then it’s understood that we start for this line here tomorrow morning at seven,” said Burton.

They all assented.

“Now, boys, let’s have some whisky,” said the general, and the conference resolved itself into a committee of the whole.

===Chinese===
“方案就是这样，诸位，”将军说，“你们意下如何？”于是他开始逐一征询在场的将官和校官们的意见，从最年轻的问起，结果谁也提不出一条建议来。

“那么就这么定了，明天早晨七点，向这里这条战线出发。”伯顿说。

众人一致点头应允。

“来吧，小伙子们，喝点威士忌。”将军说，会议随即自行改组为全体委员会。

===Original===
Early in the morning the troops began to move forward. Sam, who acted as aide-de-camp, was sent out from headquarters once or twice to urge the various colonels to make haste, but there seemed to be no special orders as to the details of the movement. The regiments went as best they could and selected their own roads, finally choosing the positions that seemed most desirable to their commanders, who took care not to leave too great an interval between regiments. The men were set to work at once at putting up the tents and making entrenchments. It was some time after midday when the general and his staff finally left the headquarters in the city. Sam came downstairs to mount his horse, and was surprised to see a landau with two horses drawn up at the door.

“Who’s that for?” he cried.

“For the general,” answered Major Stroud quietly.

“For the general! Why on earth doesn’t he ride a horse?”

“There isn’t a horse in the place that can carry him. He tried one when he first came here. He mounted it on a stepladder, and the beast came down on his knees on the stone pavement and had to be shot. He hasn’t tried it since.”

===Chinese===
清晨，部队开始向前开进。萨姆担任副官，有一两回从司令部被派出去，催促各位团长加快速度，但关于行动的细节，似乎并没有什么特别的命令。各团自行其是，自己挑路走，最后各自选定了在各自指挥官看来最称心的位置，只是留心不使团与团之间空出太大的间隔。士兵们随即动手搭帐篷、挖堑壕。过了晌午好一阵子，将军才带着参谋们终于离开城里的司令部。萨姆下楼准备上马，却吃惊地看见门口停着一辆双驾四轮大马车。

“那是给谁坐的？”他叫道。

“给将军坐的。”斯特劳德少校平静地答道。

“给将军坐的！他干吗不骑马呢？”

“这地方没有一匹马驮得动他。他刚来的时候试过一回。他是踩着梯凳上去的，那牲口当场在石头路面上扑跪下去，伤了膝盖，只得把它打死。打那以后，他就再没试过。”

===Original===
After waiting on the street for a long time Sam had the privilege of seeing the general emerge from the palace and enter his carriage. He was perspiring and fanning as usual, but carried no whisky and soda. The staff officers, of whom there were a dozen or more, mounted and followed the carriage. Sam rode next to Stroud. There was much confusion in the roads which they traveled﻿—wagons laden with tents and provisions and hospital stores, camp-followers of all descriptions, and some belated soldiers besides. The general, however, had the right of way, and they proceeded with reasonable speed. They passed through native villages, rows of one- and two-story thatched houses on each side, with wooden palisades in front of them, well shaded by low but spreading palms. They passed large sugar refineries, built by the Castalians, and churches and convents. They passed rice-fields, some covered with water and others more or less dry, which sturdy peasants were busy harrowing with buffaloes. On the road they saw many two-wheeled carts drawn by single buffaloes, the man standing in the cart as he drove. At last they came to a halt on rising ground at the edge of a piece of woodland, and Colonel Burton, the adjutant-general, rode up beside the general’s carriage and dismounted, and the two began to study the map again. After a long discussion the procession moved on again and finally stopped at the crest of a ridge, where the general alighted and soon selected a place for his tent. An hour had passed before the tents and baggage arrived, but notwithstanding the delay the tents were pitched and supper ready by sundown, and Sam found himself actually in the field on the eve of a battle. The eve, however, was somewhat prolonged. Several days passed, and Sam was kept pretty busy in riding to the various brigade and regimental headquarters and finding out how things were progressing: what was the state of the trenches, and what news there was from the enemy. Scouting parties were sent out, but their reports were kept secret, and Sam was left in the dark. There was a native village about half a mile to the rear, and the inhabitants were all friendly. Sam stopped there occasionally for a drink of water, and became acquainted with the keeper of the café, who was particularly amicable and fond of conversation. Cleary was on the lookout for accommodations in the neighborhood, and Sam introduced him to this native, Señor Garcia, who provided him with a room. One evening Sam was sitting with Cleary in the café when Garcia, as was his custom, joined them, and they began to talk in the Castalian language.

===Chinese===
在街上久等之后，萨姆有幸目睹将军从宫里出来，登上了他的马车。他照旧汗涔涔地扇着扇子，只是没带威士忌兑苏打。参谋军官们——总共有十来个或者更多——纷纷上马，跟在马车后面。萨姆骑马走在斯特劳德旁边。一路上道路拥挤不堪——满载帐篷、粮秣和医院物资的大车，形形色色随营的杂役小贩，还有几个掉队的士兵。不过将军享有优先通行权，一行人以还算说得过去的速度前进。他们穿过一个个当地人的村庄，两旁是一排排一两层高的茅草顶房子，房前立着木栅栏，低矮而枝叶舒展的棕榈树洒下浓荫。他们经过几座卡斯塔利亚人建造的大糖厂，还有教堂和修道院。他们经过一片片稻田，有的覆着水，有的较为干涸，健壮的农夫正赶着水牛忙着耙田。路上他们见到许多由单头水牛拉的两轮车，赶车人站在车里驾车。最后，他们在一处树林边缘的高地上停了下来，副官长伯顿上校骑马赶到将军的马车旁边，翻身下马，两人又开始研究起地图来。经过长长的商议，队伍重新开动，最后停在一道山脊的顶上，将军下了车，很快选定了一处扎帐篷的地方。又过了一个钟头，帐篷和行李才运到，但尽管耽搁，日落之前帐篷已经支好，晚饭也已齐备，萨姆发觉自己当真置身战场，大战在即了。然而这个“前夜”未免拖得长了些。一连过去好几天，萨姆忙得不可开交，骑马奔波于各旅各团的指挥部之间，打听事情进展如何：堑壕修得怎样了，敌方有什么动静。侦察队派出去了，可他们的报告都秘而不宣，萨姆被蒙在鼓里。后方约半英里处有一个当地人的村庄，村民个个友善。萨姆偶尔在那里歇脚讨口水喝，结识了那家咖啡馆的店主——此人格外和气，又爱攀谈。克利里正在这附近寻觅落脚的地方，萨姆便把他引荐给这位当地人塞尼奥尔·加西亚（Señor Garcia），加西亚给他腾了一间房。一天傍晚，萨姆正同克利里坐在咖啡馆里，加西亚照他的老习惯凑了过来，三人便用卡斯塔利亚语交谈起来。

===Original===
“We are glad you people are coming to rule our islands,” said Garcia; “that is, those of us who know your history, because we know that you are a great people and love freedom.”

“I am pleased to hear it,” said Sam. “Cleary, I was sure that all the sensible natives would feel that way.”

“You believe in liberty, equality, fraternity?”

“Of course we do,” said Cleary.

“Yes,” said Sam, “if you understand those words properly. Now liberty doesn’t interfere with obedience. Our whole army here is built up on the idea of obedience. We’ve all got liberty, of course, but﻿—”

===Chinese===
“你们这些人就要来统治我们的岛屿了，我们很高兴，”加西亚说，“至少我们这些了解你们历史的人是高兴的，因为我们知道你们是一个伟大的民族，热爱自由。”

“听你这么说我真高兴，”萨姆说，“克利里，我原先就笃定，凡属通情达理的土人，都会这么想。”

“你们信奉自由、平等、博爱？”

“我们当然信奉。”克利里说。

“是的，”萨姆说，“前提是你要把这些字眼理解得恰当。自由嘛，同服从并不冲突。我们这里的整支军队，就是建立在服从这个观念上的。我们人人当然都有自由，不过——”

===Original===
“Liberty to do what?” asked Garcia innocently.

“Why, liberty to﻿—well, to﻿—yes, liberty to do as we’re ordered,” said Sam.

“Ah! I see,” said Garcia. “And then you have equality.”

“Yes,” said Sam, “in a general way we have. But that doesn’t prevent people from differing in rank. Now there’s the general, he’s my superior, and I’m the superior of the lieutenants, and we’re all superior to the privates. We have regular schools at home to teach us not to misunderstand the kind of equality that we believe in. There’s one at East Point for the army. This gentleman and I were educated there. We weren’t allowed even to look at our superiors. There’s another institution like it for the navy. And then every man-of-war and every army garrison is a sort of college to spread these ideas about rank. A captain of a ship can’t even let his officers dine with him too often. It’s a fine system and it prevents us from making any mistakes about what equality means.”

“And then fraternity?” asked Garcia.

“Oh, that’s just the same,” said Cleary. “At East Point we got a blow in the jaw if we showed the wrong kind of fraternity to our betters.”

===Chinese===
“自由去做什么的自由呢？”加西亚天真地问。

“这个嘛，自由去——呃，去——对了，自由去按命令行事。”萨姆说。

“啊！我明白了，”加西亚说，“然后你们还有平等。”

“是的，”萨姆说，“大体而言，我们是平等的。可这并不妨碍人们等级有别。譬如将军，他是我的上级；我又是中尉们的上级；而我们大家又都是列兵的上级。我们在国内有正规的学堂，专门教我们别把我们信奉的那种平等理解岔了。陆军有一所在东点。这位先生和我就是在那里受的教育。我们连抬头看上级都不被允许。海军另有一所同样的学府。再说，每一艘军舰、每一处陆军驻地，都算得一所传播这套等级观念的大学。一艘军舰的舰长，甚至不能让手下的军官同他一起用饭用得太勤。这套制度妙极了，保准我们不会在平等的含义上出半点差错。”

“那么博爱呢？”加西亚问。

“哦，那也是一路的，”克利里说，“在东点，我们要是对长官表示了不该有的那种亲热，下巴上就得挨一拳。”

===Original===
“It’s a wonderful system,” said Garcia. “But I have heard some of your people explain liberty, equality, fraternity a little differently.”

“They must have been civilians,” said Sam. “The army and navy represent all that is best in our country, and the people at large do not understand the army and navy. Luckily for you, the islands will be in charge of the army. There won’t be any mistake about the kind of liberty and equality we give you.”

“I am so grateful,” said Garcia, rolling up his eyes.

“Yes, Cleary,” said Sam. “The people at home don’t understand us. Did you see that there’s a bill in Congress to allow men in the ranks, mere noncommissioned officers, to apply for commissions? If they pass it, it will be the end of the army. Just think of a sergeant becoming one of us! Oh, I forgot, you aren’t an officer, but you must know how I feel!”

===Chinese===
“这制度真是了不起，”加西亚说，“可我听过你们有些人解释自由、平等、博爱，说法稍稍不同。”

“那他们准是平民，”萨姆说，“陆军和海军代表着我们国家一切最优秀的品质，而老百姓并不理解陆军和海军。算你们走运，这几座岛屿将交由陆军掌管。我们要给你们的那种自由与平等，保准错不了。”

“真是感激不尽。”加西亚说着，两眼朝上一翻。

“可不是嘛，克利里，”萨姆说，“国内的老百姓不理解我们。你看到没有，国会有一项法案，竟要允许行伍出身的士兵——区区军士——申请军官委任？要是通过了，陆军就算完蛋了。想想看，一个中士居然要变成我们这样的人！哦，我忘了，你不是军官，不过你一定明白我是什么滋味！”

===Original===
Cleary expressed his sympathy, and Sam bade him and his host good night. On his way back through a path in the jungle he thought he heard a light step behind him, but when he looked back he could see nothing. When he arrived at the headquarters’ tent he found all the higher officers of the army there, and Stroud whispered to him that they had heard that Gomaldo would take the offensive the next morning, and that consequently a general advance was ordered for daybreak in order that they might forestall him. The general was rather taken by surprise and his final plans were not ready, but it was arranged that at four o’clock each regiment should advance, and that orders containing further details would be sent to them by six o’clock at the latest. Burton remained in the general’s tent to perfect the orders, and Sam went to the tent which he occupied with Major Stroud to enjoy a few hours’ sleep.

“I’m afraid we’re not quite ready,” said Sam.

“No army ever is,” replied Stroud laconically.

“I wish the general were a little livelier and quicker,” said Sam, blushing at his own blasphemy.

“And thinner?” said Stroud, smiling, as he twisted his white mustache and smoothed his imperial. “Oh, he’ll do very well. He’s a good solid point to rally round and fall back on, and then we always know where to find him, for he can’t get away very far if he tries.”

===Chinese===
克利里表示了一番同情，萨姆便向他和主人道了晚安。在循着一条丛林小径返回的路上，他觉得身后似乎有个轻捷的脚步声，可回头望时又什么也看不见。到了司令部帐篷，他发现陆军的高级军官全都在那里，斯特劳德悄声告诉他：他们得到消息，戈马尔多明天早晨就要发动攻势，因此已经下令全军于拂晓总攻，好抢先一步制住他。将军颇有些措手不及，最后的方案还没有备妥，但已经商定：四点钟各团一齐推进，载有进一步细节的命令最迟六点发到各团。伯顿留在将军帐里把命令定稿，萨姆则回到他与斯特劳德少校合住的帐篷里，享受几个钟头的睡眠。

“恐怕咱们还没完全准备好。”萨姆说。

“没有一支军队是完全准备好的。”斯特劳德言简意赅地答道。

“我倒希望将军能稍微精神些、麻利些。”萨姆说，为自己这句大不敬的话红了脸。

“再瘦些？”斯特劳德捻着雪白的髭须，抹了抹唇下的短须，微笑道，“哦，他蛮可以的。他是个结结实实的靠山，集结也围得着他，退守也靠得着他；再说咱们永远知道上哪儿去找他，因为他就是想跑，也跑不了多远。”

===Original===
At half-past three in the morning the officers of the staff were called by a native servant and began to make their preparations. They breakfasted as best they could on coffee without sugar or cream, and some stale bread, with an egg apiece, and whisky. Sam felt unaccountably sleepy, and he thought that all the rest looked sleepy too. It was five o’clock before Burton had the orders ready for the various subordinate commanders, telling each of them in which direction to advance. The plan had been mapped out the night before, but the orders had to be copied and corrected. At last he came out and distributed them to Stroud, Sam, and several other officers﻿—two orders to each, yawning painfully as he handed them out.

“I don’t think I slept a wink last night,” he said.

===Chinese===
凌晨三点半，一名当地仆役叫醒了参谋部的军官们，众人开始整备。早饭是将就着吃的：不加糖也不加奶油的咖啡、几块陈面包，每人一个鸡蛋，还有威士忌。萨姆觉得困得出奇，又看其余人人人都像没睡醒的样子。直到五点钟，伯顿才把发给各下属指挥官的命令准备停当，命令告诉每位指挥官该朝哪个方向推进。方案头天夜里就拟定好了，可命令还得誊抄、修改。末了他走了出来，把命令分发给斯特劳德、萨姆和另外几名军官——每人两份——一边分发一边苦打着哈欠。

“我看我昨天夜里连一眨眼的工夫都没睡着。”他说。

===Original===
The two commands to which Sam’s orders were directed were stationed on the extreme right of the army. He made a rough tracing of that part of the map and set out at once on a wiry little native pony. For some distance he followed the high road, but then was obliged to turn into a branch road which led through the woods, and which soon became a mere wood-path. Before long he heard firing in front of him, and soon he recognized the sound of whistling bullets above his head. He found himself ducking his head involuntarily, and almost for the first time in his life he was conscious of being afraid. This was a surprise to him, as his thoughts during the night whenever he had been awake had been full of pleasant anticipations.

The path suddenly came out into an open rolling country, and Sam pulled up his horse, dismounted, and hiding behind some underbrush, took a look at the situation. There was a Gatling-gun, worked by a young officer and five men, a few hundred yards to the right at the edge of the woods. Beyond to the front he could see a line of troops firing at the enemy from behind a wall. Of the Cubapinos he could see nothing but the smoke of their guns and muskets here and there. Shells were falling in another part of the field, but nowhere near him. Bullets were flying thick through the air, and he heard them hissing constantly. As he looked he saw one of the Gatling crew fall over, doubled up in a heap. Sam moved along in the wood nearer to this gun, so that he might ask where he could find the brigade commander. As he approached he heard the lieutenant say:

“Damn those sharpshooters. They’ve got our range now. With this damned smokeless powder they can pick us all off. Clark, bring some of that artificial smoke stuff here.”

The soldier obeyed, and in a few moments a dense smoke rose above them, covering the whole neighborhood.

“What a wonderful thing these inventions are!” thought Sam, as he tied his horse to a tree and advanced crouching toward the battery. The lieutenant pointed out to him the position of the brigadier-general, some distance back on the right under cover of the jungle, and told him of a path that would take him there. Sam was not slow to follow his directions, for just then a shell exploded close by. He soon found the general surrounded by his staff on a partially wooded hill, from which, however, they could command the field with their glasses. Bullets were flying about them, and an occasional shell sailed over their heads, but the general seemed perfectly at home. He took the orders, opened them and read them.

===Chinese===
萨姆那两道命令所要送达的两支部队，驻在全军最右翼。他把地图上那一部分粗粗描了一份，随即骑上一匹精瘦的当地小马出发了。有一段路他顺着大路走，随后不得不拐进一条穿林的岔路，那岔路不多时便成了一条林间小径。没走多远，他听见前方响起了枪声，很快又听出头顶上有子弹呼啸而过。他发觉自己不由自主地把头一缩——平生几乎头一回，他意识到自己在害怕。这让他颇感意外，因为夜里醒着的那些时候，他满脑子都是愉快的期待。

小径忽然穿出林子，眼前是一片开阔的丘陵地。萨姆勒住马，跳下来，躲在一丛灌木后面观察形势。右边几百码外的林缘，有一挺加特林机枪，由一名年轻军官和五名士兵操作着。再往前，他望见一线部队正依托一道墙向敌人射击。至于古巴平人（Cubapinos），他只看得见他们的火炮和步枪这儿那儿冒出的硝烟。炮弹正落在战场的另一处，离他远远的。子弹在空中飞得又密又急，他耳朵里尽是子弹的嘶嘶声。正看着，他瞧见加特林炮组里有一名士兵扑地栽倒，蜷作一团。萨姆在林中朝那挺机枪挪近了些，好打听旅长在什么地方。走近时，他听见那名中尉说道：

“该死的狙击手。他们已经测准咱们的距离了。用这种该死的无烟火药，他们能把咱们一个个都点名干掉。克拉克（Clark），把那种人造烟幕的东西拿些过来。”

士兵领命照办，不多时，一股浓烟在他们上方腾起，把整片地方都罩住了。

“这些发明真是了不起！”萨姆一面想，一面把马拴在树上，猫着腰朝机枪阵地推进。中尉给他指了准将的位置——在右后方不远处，有丛林遮蔽——又告诉他一条能通到那里的小径。萨姆毫不迟疑地照他指的路走，因为恰在此时，一发炮弹在近旁炸开了。他很快找到了那位将军，将军正被参谋们簇拥在一座半为林木覆盖的小山上，不过从那里，他们用望远镜倒能将战场尽收眼底。子弹在他们四周乱飞，偶尔还有一发炮弹从头顶上掠过，将军却是一副安然自在的样子。他接过命令，拆开来读。

===Original===
“That’s strange,” said he. “Last night I understood that I was to make for that pass between the hills there on the left, and now I’m ordered to take the first turning to the right. I don’t understand it. Do you know anything about it?”

“No, sir.”

“Well, he must have changed his mind. Or else it was a bluff to keep his plans from leaking out. Tell the general that I will carry out his orders at once.”

===Chinese===
“这就怪了，”他说，“昨天晚上我领会的意思，是朝左边那两座山之间的隘口推进，现在却命令我在第一个路口右转。我闹不明白。你知道是怎么回事吗？”

“不知道，长官。”

“唔，他准是改变主意了。要不然，就是放的一颗烟幕弹，免得他的计划泄露出去。请转告将军，我立刻执行他的命令。”

===Original===
Sam inquired of the members of the staff where he would be likely to find the 43rd Volunteers, to whose colonel his other orders were directed, but they had no information, except that in the morning that regiment had been stationed farther over on the right. Sam started out again, guiding himself as best he could by a compass which he had in his pocket. He selected the paths which seemed most promising, but the jungle between was impenetrable on horseback. The firing on the extreme right seemed to be farther in the rear, and he made his way in that direction. Again he came out at the edge of the woods, and to his surprise saw a battalion of the enemy at a short distance from him. He turned his horse, stuck his spurs into him, and went back along the path to the rear at a full run, while a shower of bullets fell around him. He still kept on working to the right in the direction of the firing which he heard in front of him. At last in a hollow of the jungle he came upon a Red Cross station, one of those advance temporary relief posts where the wounded who are too much injured to be taken at once to the rear are treated. Twenty or thirty men were lying in a row, some of them on their coats, others on the bare ground. Two surgeons were doing what they could in the line of first aid to the injured, binding up arms and legs, dressing wounds, and trying to stop the flow of blood from arteries. Two soldiers were lifting a wounded man on a stretcher so that he might be carried to the rear, and he was groaning with agony. Every one of the patients was blotched in one place or another with blood, and some of them were lying in pools of the crimson fluid. Sam felt a little sick at his stomach. Two men came in with another stretcher, bringing a wounded man from the front. The man gave a convulsive start as they set him down.

===Chinese===
萨姆向参谋们打听，他另一道命令所致的对方——第四十三志愿团（43rd Volunteers）的团长——可能在哪里，可他们都说不出个所以然，只知道早晨该团驻扎在更靠右翼的地方。萨姆再度出发，全凭衣袋里的一只罗盘尽量辨认方向。他专挑看来最有指望的小径走，可小径之间的丛林骑马根本穿不过去。最右翼的枪声似乎退向更后方了，他便朝那个方向前进。他又一次走到林缘，吃惊地发现不远处有一个营的敌军。他拨转马头，马刺往马身上一磕，沿着小径朝后方一路飞奔，一阵弹雨噼里啪啦落在他周围。他仍旧继续向右，朝前方传来的枪声摸去。最后，他在丛林的一处洼地里撞见了一个红十字救护站——那种前沿临时救护点，伤势太重、无法立刻送到后方去的伤员，就在这种地方救治。二三十个人一排躺着，有的躺在自己的军衣上，有的直接躺在光地上。两名军医正尽力施以急救：包扎手臂和大腿，敷裹伤口，设法止住动脉涌出的血。两名士兵正把一个伤员抬上担架，准备送往后方，那伤员痛得不住呻吟。每个伤员身上都或多或少沾着斑斑血污，有几个干脆躺在一滩滩殷红的血泊里。萨姆觉得胃里一阵发呕。又有两个人抬着担架进来，从前线送下来一个伤员。放下的当口，那人猛地抽搐了一下。

===Original===
“A bullet’s just hit him in the head,” said one of the men. “I’m glad it wasn’t me.”

One of the doctors looked at the wounded man.

“He’s dead,” he said. “Damn you, what do you mean by bringing dead men here?”

The two bearers took up their load again and dropped it out of sight in the bushes. Sam did not like to interrupt the doctors, who were overtasked, so he dismounted and tried to find a wounded man well enough to answer his questions. One man at the end of the row looked less pale than the rest, and he asked him where he could find the 43d.

===Chinese===
“一颗子弹刚打中了他的脑袋，”其中一人说，“幸好不是我。”

一名医生看了看那伤员。

“他死了，”他说，“你们这些混蛋，抬死人来干什么？”

两名担架员重新抬起他们的担子，把它抬到灌木丛后看不见的地方撂下了。萨姆不愿打搅那两位早已应接不暇的军医，便下了马，想找一个伤势还轻、能答话的伤员。排尾有一个人瞧着比别人没那么苍白，萨姆就问他，第四十三团在哪儿能找到。

===Original===
“That’s my regiment, sir,” he replied, as a twig, cut off by a bullet, fell on his face. “You’d better lie down here, sir; you’ll be shot if you don’t. A lot of the wounded have been hit here again.”

Sam sat down by his side.

“Our regiment is over that way,” he said, pointing in the direction of the firing. “I don’t know where the colonel is. We haven’t seen him for hours. The lieutenant-colonel is down with fever. I think the major’s in command. You ought to find him at the front. We’ve been falling back, and the firing sounds nearer than it did. I’m afraid the enemy will catch us here.”

Sam did not wait to hear anything further, but, leaving his horse tied to a tree, he ran toward the front. He found many soldiers skulking along the path, and they directed him to the major. He discovered him sitting on the ground behind a stone wall.

===Chinese===
“那就是我的团，长官，”他答道，正说话间，一截被子弹削断的小树枝落在他的脸上。“您最好也在这儿躺下，长官；不躺下会挨枪子儿的。好多伤员在这儿又挨了枪。”

萨姆在他身边坐了下来。

“我们团在那边，”他指着枪声的方向说，“上校在哪儿，我不知道。我们好几个钟头没见着他了。中校发热病倒了。我想现在掌管指挥的是少校。您到前线该能找到他。我们一直在后撤，枪声听着比先前近了。我怕敌人会在这儿追上我们。”

萨姆不等再听下去，把马拴在一棵树上，便朝前线跑去。他看见许多士兵沿着小径躲躲闪闪地溜，他们给他指了少校的所在。他发现少校正坐在一道石墙后面的地上。

===Original===
“Here, major, are your orders. I understand you’re in command.”

“Not much,” said the major. “The colonel’s in command. You’d better find him.”

“Where is he?”

“I’m sure I don’t know. I haven’t seen him since six o’clock.”

“But this is your regiment, isn’t it?”

“Well, yes. It’s part of it.”

===Chinese===
“喂，少校，这是给你们的命令。我听说现在由您指挥。”

“哪儿的话，”少校说，“指挥的是上校。您还是去找他吧。”

“他在哪儿？”

“我实在说不上来。六点钟以后我就没见过他。”

“可这是您的团啊，不是吗？”

“呃，是吧。算是这个团的一部分。”

===Original===
Just then a young captain came running up from the front, and cried out to his major:

“Major, we’re having a hard time of it there. Won’t you come up and take charge? I’m afraid they’ll force us back.”

“No,” said the major, “I won’t. I’m going back there to that last village. It’s a much better place to defend. Besides I’m not feeling well. You fellows can stay here if you like. I shan’t order the regiment back, but I’ll go back and get ready for them there. We ought to have trenches there, you know,” and he got up and walked rapidly off down the road. The captain turned to Sam.

===Chinese===
正在这时，一名年轻上尉从前线跑上来，冲着他的少校嚷道：

“少校，我们那边顶得很吃力。您能不能上来掌掌舵？我怕他们要把我们压回来了。”

“不，”少校说，“我不去。我要退回后面那个村子去。那地方好守得多。再说，我也觉着不舒服。你们几位乐意的话就留在这儿好了。我不会下令全团后撤，可我自己要先退回去，在那边替你们打点停当。那儿本来就该挖好堑壕，你们知道。”说着他站起身，顺着大路匆匆走了。那上尉转向萨姆。

===Original===
“I beg your pardon, captain,” said he, “but what are we to do? Our officers have given out, and we’re a new regiment and haven’t any experience. Won’t you take command?”

Sam was by no means satisfied in his mind that he would behave much better than the major, but here was an opportunity that he could not afford to lose.

“I’ll see what I can do,” said he. “Let’s see what the orders are.”

He opened the document and saw that it was a direction to keep on to the front until they arrived before the town of San Diego, which they were to assault and capture.

“Show me where your men are,” said Sam. “Who have you got there?”

“We’ve got our own regiment, the 43rd, and six or eight companies of the 72nd﻿—I don’t know where they came from; and then there’s a battery, and perhaps some others.”

===Chinese===
“对不起，上尉，”他说，“可我们眼下该怎么办呢？我们的军官都不顶用了，我们又是新团，毫无经验。您愿不愿意接手指挥？”

萨姆心里一点也拿不准，自己会不会比那位少校强多少，可这是个千载难逢、绝不能错过的机会。

“我来看看我能做什么，”他说，“先瞧瞧命令上怎么说。”

他拆开命令，见上面是一道指示：继续向前推进，直到兵临圣迭戈（San Diego）城下，将该城强攻夺占。

“带我去看看你们的人在哪儿，”萨姆说，“那儿都有哪些部队？”

“有我们自己的团，第四十三团，还有第七十二团的六到八个连——他们打哪儿来的，我不知道；另外有一个炮兵连，兴许还有些别的。”

===Original===
They hastened along the road together, urging the stragglers to join them, which many of them did. The way became more and more encumbered with men, and the bullets came thicker. Sam was thoroughly scared. He could feel his legs waver at the knee, and it seemed as if a giant hand had grasped him by the spine. They passed several musicians of the band.

“Start up a tune!” cried Sam. “Play something and follow us.” At the same time he instinctively thrust his hand into his breast pocket and felt for his traveling Lares and Penates, namely, his tin soldier, his photographs of East Point, one of Marian, and her last letter. Meanwhile the band began to play and the bass-drummer wielded his huge drumstick with all his might. Sam began to feel happier, and so did the men about him. One of the musicians suddenly fell, struck dead by a bullet, and just then a shell burst over them and two or three men went down. With one accord the soldiers began to curse and swear in the most frightful manner and to insist on speedy vengeance. Sam was surprised to find himself enjoying the oaths. They just expressed his feelings, and he hurried on to the edge of the woods. In front of them they saw a line of their own men lying on the ground behind stones and logs, shooting at the enemy, whose line could be distinguished hardly more than a third of a mile away.

“They’re nearer than they were,” whispered the captain. “We must push them back or they’ll have us. The men on the firing line are getting scared.”

“We must scare them behind more than the enemy does in front,” said Sam, drawing his revolver. “Here you, sir, get back into your place.”

A man in the ranks, who was beginning to creep back, saw the revolver and dropped back in his position with an oath.

===Chinese===
两人一道沿着大路疾进，一路招呼掉队的士兵跟上来，果真有许多人归了队。路上的人越聚越多，子弹也越来越密。萨姆怕到了极点。他觉出自己两条腿在膝盖处直打晃，仿佛有一只巨手攥住了他的脊梁。他们路过军乐队的几名乐手。

“奏个曲子！”萨姆喊道，“随便奏点什么，跟着我们前进！”与此同时，他本能地把手伸进胸前的口袋，去摸他那些随身的家宅守护神（Lares and Penates）——也就是他的铅兵、他的几张东点照片、一张玛丽安的照片，还有她最近的一封信。乐声随即奏响，大鼓手抡起粗大的鼓槌，使出浑身力气擂着。萨姆渐渐觉得心里松快了些，他周围的人也是如此。一名乐手突然栽倒，被一颗子弹当场打死；恰在此时，一发炮弹在他们头顶上炸开，又有两三个人倒了下去。士兵们不约而同地破口大骂起来，骂得惊天动地，一致叫嚷着要立刻复仇。萨姆惊觉自己竟听得津津有味。那些咒骂恰恰道出了他的心情，他加紧脚步赶向林边。只见前方自己人一线排开，卧在石头和圆木后面朝敌人射击，敌人的战线隐约可辨，离着至多不过三分之一英里。

“他们比先前逼近了，”上尉低声道，“咱们得把他们顶回去，不然他们就吃掉咱们了。火线上的弟兄开始发慌了。”

“那咱们就得让身后吓唬他们的，比前头打他们的敌人更狠。”萨姆说着，拔出左轮手枪，“喂，你，先生，给我回到你的位置上去。”

队伍里有一个正朝后爬的士兵，一见左轮枪，骂了一声，乖乖缩回了原地。

===Original===
“Forward!” cried Sam, now thoroughly in the spirit of the occasion. “Come up to the front, all of you, and extend our line there to the right. Lie down and take careful aim with every shot.”

The men did as they were told, and Sam took up his position behind the line with the captain, both of them standing in a perfect gale of bullets, while all the rest were lying down.

“Lie down,” said Sam to the captain. “You’ve no business to risk your life like that.”

“How about yours, sir?” said the captain, as he obeyed.

“I’ll take care of myself, if you’ll be good enough to let me,” answered Sam.

===Chinese===
“前进！”萨姆喊道，此刻他已完全进入了这场合的精神，“统统到前面来，把咱们的战线往右边延展。卧倒，每一枪都瞄准了再打。”

士兵们照办了，萨姆同那上尉在战线后面占了位——两人在一阵十足的弹雨狂风里笔直站着，其余的人却全都卧倒着。

“卧倒，”萨姆对上尉说，“你可犯不着这样拿性命去冒险。”

“那您自己的呢，长官？”上尉一边照办一边说。

“只要您行行好肯由着我，我自会当心自己。”萨姆答道。

===Original===
The presence of a staff officer gave new courage to the men, and their marksmanship began to have effect on the enemy, who were seen to be gradually falling back. Sam took this opportunity to move his line forward, and he sent a lieutenant to direct the battery to cover his men when they should charge on the enemy’s line. He moved his line forward in this way successively three or four times, and the troops were now thoroughly encouraged, and some of them even asked to be allowed to charge. Sam, however, postponed this final act as long as he could. It was not until he saw the captain whom he had met in the woods mangled and instantly killed by a piece of shell that he became so angry that he could restrain himself no longer. He gave the order to fix bayonets, and with a yell the men rose from their lairs and rushed over the intervening ground to the enemy’s position. The Cubapinos did not wait for them, but turned and ran precipitously. Sam and his men followed them for at least a mile, when they made a stand again.

===Chinese===
参谋军官的到场给士兵们平添了新的勇气，他们的枪法渐渐在敌人身上见了效，眼看着敌军逐步后撤。萨姆趁机把战线推前，又派一名中尉去通知炮兵连，等部队向敌军战线冲锋时以炮火掩护。他就这样接连把战线推进了三四次，部队如今士气大振，有些人甚至主动请求准许冲锋。萨姆却把这最后一手能拖多久拖多久。直到他眼见他先前在林间遇上的那位上尉被一块弹片搅得血肉横飞、当场毙命，他才怒火中烧，再也无法自持。他下令上刺刀，士兵们随着一声呐喊从各自伏身之处一跃而起，冲过中间那段开阔地，直扑敌军阵地。古巴平人并不等他们赶到，掉头便没命地溃逃。萨姆带着人追赶了至少一英里，敌人才重新稳住阵脚。

===Original===
“They’re in the trenches now that they were in this morning,” explained a lieutenant.

Here the same tactics were renewed, and in another half-hour Sam ordered his men to charge again. This time the enemy waited longer, and many of the attacking party fell, but before they reached the trenches the Cubapinos took flight, and Sam saw his soldiers bayonet the last two or three of them in the back. There were a good many dead in the trenches, all of them shot through the head. It was a proud moment for Sam when he stood on the edge of the trench and planted Old Gory there while the men cheered. A wounded Cubapino lay just before him, and one of the soldiers kicked him in the head and killed him. Sam noticed it, and was a little startled to find that it seemed all right to him.

“I’ve half a mind to kick the next wounded man I see,” he thought. “It must be rather good sport”; but he did not do it.

===Chinese===
“他们现在占据的，就是今天早晨他们待过的那几条堑壕。”一名中尉解释道。

老一套战法再来了一遍，又过了半个钟头，萨姆下令再度冲锋。这一回敌人坚持得久些，进攻一方倒下了不少人，可还没等他们冲到堑壕，古巴平人又飞跑了，萨姆眼见他的士兵把最后两三个敌人从背后捅死。堑壕里横七竖八躺着不少尸体，个个都是头部中弹。萨姆站在堑壕边缘，把老光荣旗（Old Gory）插在那儿，士兵们齐声欢呼——这是他得意已极的一刻。一个负伤的古巴平人正好躺在他面前，一名士兵朝那脑袋上踢了一脚，把他踢死了。萨姆看在眼里，心里微微一惊——却惊觉这在他看来竟没有什么不对。

“我倒有几分想踢下一个见到的伤兵，”他心想，“那想必挺好玩”；不过他没有这么做。

===Original===
The rest of the fight was in the nature of a procession. They pursued the flying Cubapinos as fast as they could, but were unable to come up with them. In a native village through which they passed, Sam asked an old man, who had been too weak to get away, how far off San Diego was, and learned that it was five miles away to the left. He could not understand this, but still he kept on in that direction. As they left the village it burst into flames, for the last soldiers had set it on fire. Sam thought of the old man perishing in his hut, and it seemed to him a fine thing and quite natural. On their way they came across other bodies of troops who joined them, and it so happened that no one came forward of superior rank to Sam, and consequently he retained the command. Before they came in sight of San Diego he had quite a brigade under him. He halted them in front of the town and sent out a scouting party. There was no sound of firing now except in the distance. In an hour the scouting party came back and reported that the place had been vacated by the enemy, who for some reason had been seized by a panic. Sam ordered the advance to be resumed, and late in the afternoon found himself in possession of San Diego. He began to take measures at once to fortify the place, when the brigadier-general whom he had seen in the morning marched in with his brigade and took over the command from him, congratulating him on his success, which was already the talk of the army. Sam turned over the command to him with much grace and dignity, and, borrowing a horse, set off for the old headquarters which he had left in the morning, for he learned that, although the enemy were completely defeated and scattered, still the general would not move his headquarters forward to the front till the following day.

===Chinese===
接下来的战斗，性质已近乎一场游行。他们竭尽全力追赶溃逃的古巴平人，却怎么也追不上。途经一座当地人的村庄时，萨姆问一个衰弱得来不及逃走的老汉，圣迭戈还有多远，得知它在左面五英里之外。他闹不明白这是怎么回事，却仍旧朝着那个方向前进。他们离开村子时，村子腾起了大火，因为走在最后的我方士兵放了火。萨姆想到那位老汉就要葬身在他自己的小屋里，只觉得这景象很壮美，而且再自然不过。一路上他们又遇上别的部队加入进来，偏巧没有军衔高于萨姆的人出面，于是指挥权仍旧归他执掌。还没望见圣迭戈，他手底下已经有足足一个旅了。他在城前让部队停下，派出一支侦察队。此刻除了远处，四下里再听不见枪声。一个钟头后，侦察队回来报告：敌军已经弃城而去，不知出于什么缘故，他们陷于一场恐慌。萨姆下令继续前进，傍晚时分，他发觉圣迭戈已在自己手中。他当即着手布置筑垒设防，恰在此时，他早晨见到的那位准将带着全旅开了进来，从他手里接过指挥权，并向他道贺——他的战功早已传遍全军，成了人人谈论的话题。萨姆风度翩翩、体面堂皇地交出了指挥权，借来一匹马，动身返回他清晨离开的老司令部去，因为他听说，敌军虽已被彻底击溃打散，将军却要到第二天才肯把司令部推进到前沿。

===Original===
The general received him with great cordiality.

“Everything turned out just as I planned it,” he said, “but, Captain, you helped us out at a critical point there on the right. I shall mention you in despatches. You may depend on being promoted and given a good post. You ought to have a regiment at least.”

Sam was taking his supper when Cleary came in, hot and grimy.

===Chinese===
将军热诚地接待了他。

“一切都完全按我的谋划实现了，”他说，“不过，上尉，在右边那个紧要关头，你可帮了大忙。我要在战报里表彰你。你尽管放心，定会得到晋升，得个好差事。起码也该统领一个团。”

萨姆正在吃晚饭，克利里闯了进来，一身热汗，满脸污灰。

===Original===
“Well, you’re a great fellow,” he said, “to get away from me the way you did this morning. But didn’t I tell you, you were the stuff? Why, you won the battle. Do you know that you turned their left flank?”

“To tell the truth, I didn’t know it,” said Sam.

“Well, you did.”

“But the general planned everything,” said Sam.

===Chinese===
“嘿，你可真了不起，”他说，“今天早晨竟那么把我甩下了。可我不是跟你说过了吗，你就是那块料！嘿，这一仗是你打赢的。你知道不知道，你端了他们的左翼？”

“说实话，我不知道。”萨姆说。

“可你确实端了。”

“可一切都是将军谋划的呀。”萨姆说。

===Original===
“Yes,” said Cleary, “but I’ll tell you more about that. I’m doing some detective work, and I’ll have something to tell you in a day or two. But I wish I’d been with you. I had my kodak all ready. However, they can make up the pictures at home. How’s this for headlines?” and he took some notes from his pocket. “ ‘Great Victory at San Diego. Captain Jinks Turns Defeat into Victory. Hailed as Hero Jinks by the Army. General Laughter’s Plans Carried Out through the Young Hero’s Cooperation.’ What do you think of that? We’ll put the part about the general in small caps, because he’s not quite solid with the trust. I’m not going to write up anybody but you and the Mounted Mustangs; those are my orders.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”克利里说，“不过这里头另有文章，我回头再跟你细说。我这几天正做些侦探的功课，过一两天就有东西告诉你。只是我真恨不得当时跟你在一处。我的柯达相机都备好了。好在照片回国后自会炮制出来。你瞧这个标题如何？”他从口袋里掏出几页笔记来。“‘圣迭戈大捷。金克斯上尉反败为胜。全军上下欢呼金克斯英雄。劳夫特将军的方略赖少年英雄襄助而克竟全功。’你看怎么样？将军那半句我们排成小号大写字母，因为他跟托拉斯的关系还不够铁。我奉命只报道你一个，外加野马骑兵团（the Mounted Mustangs）；这是上头给我的指令。”

===Original===
“How did the Mustangs make out?” asked Sam. “They were way off on the left, and I haven’t heard anything about them.”

“They did very decently,” said Cleary, “considering they were never under fire before. They kept up pretty well with the regulars, and fortunately they had a regular regiment on each side. They really did well.”

“Did they make any fine cavalry charges?” inquired Sam.

“Cavalry charges! Bless your heart, they didn’t have any horses, and it’s lucky they didn’t. They had their hands full without having to manage any horses!”

===Chinese===
“野马骑兵团打得怎么样？”萨姆问，“他们远在左翼，我一点儿消息也没听到。”

“他们打得相当体面，”克利里说，“考虑到他们以前从没上过火线。他们跟正规军跟得挺紧，幸好他们左右两翼各有一个正规团夹着。他们打得着实不坏。”

“他们有没有来几下漂亮的骑兵冲锋？”萨姆问道。

“骑兵冲锋！我的老天，他们连一匹马都没有呢，幸亏没有。不必再分神照料马匹，他们手头的事就已经够呛了！”

## XVI The End / 十六 终局

===Original===
Sam was indeed a sick man, and the journey to the East proved to be a severe strain upon him. Cleary saw that it would be unwise to let him travel alone with his wife, and accordingly he accompanied him to Slowburgh, which was on the way to Homeville. They arrived in the afternoon, and Sam could hardly walk to the carriage which awaited him. He was put to bed as soon as he reached his uncle’s house, and on the advice of his uncle’s doctor they sent at once to the county town for a trained nurse to take charge of him, for it was out of the question for him to travel farther. There was no train which Cleary could conveniently take that evening to the metropolis, and he accepted the urgent invitation of Congressman Jinks to spend the night. It so happened that it was a gala day for Slowburgh. Four of her soldier sons had returned a few days before from Porsslania and the Cubapines, and this day had been set aside for a great celebration and a mass-meeting at the Methodist church to welcome them. The procession was to take place early in the evening, and after supper Cleary went out alone to watch the proceedings, leaving his friend to the care of his relatives. He took his place on the curbstone of the principal street and was soon conversing with his neighbors on each side, one of whom was our old friend, Mr. Reddy, and the other the young insurance agent whose acquaintance Sam had made at the hotel.

===Chinese===
萨姆这回真是个病人了，这趟东行对他的身体是一场严峻的煎熬。克利里看出，让他只由妻子一人陪着赶路实为不智，便索性送他到了斯洛堡——那地方正走在去霍姆维尔的半道上。两人午后抵达，萨姆几乎走不到前来迎候的马车跟前。他一进叔叔家便被安顿上床；依叔叔那位医生的主意，家里立刻打发人到县城请了一位受过专门训练的护士来照护他，因为再要他往前赶路是万万谈不上了。当晚克利里没有便当的火车可搭回大都会，便领了国会议员金克斯的殷切挽留，留宿一夜。偏偏这一天又是斯洛堡的一个大喜日子：城里四个当兵的儿子几天前从波斯兰尼亚和古巴平回来了，这一天被定下来办盛大的庆祝，还要在卫理公会教堂开群众大会欢迎他们。游行定在傍晚头里举行；晚饭后，克利里把朋友交给亲戚照料，独自出门去看热闹。他在大街的路边石上占了个位置，不多时便同左右两侧的邻人攀谈起来——一位是我们的老朋友雷迪先生（Mr. Reddy），另一位，正是萨姆当年在旅馆里结识的那位年轻保险代理。

===Original===
“It’s going to be a great show,” said the former. “I wish I was spry enough to parade too. It’s going to be splendid, but it won’t come up to the time we had when I came back from the war. They’ve kept them four boys drunk three days for nothing, but we was drunk a month.”

“They’ve sobered them down for this evening, I believe,” said the young man.

“They’ve done their best,” said Reddy, “and I think they’ll go through with it all right. It’s a great time for them, but they’ll have their pension days all the rest of their lives to remind them of it, four times a year.”

===Chinese===
“这准是场大排场。”前者说道，“可惜我这把老骨头不够利索，不能也去游上一回。场面保管精彩，可怎么也比不上我打仗回来那回的风光。他们让那四个小子灌了三天黄汤，那算个啥；我们那会儿可是足足灌了一个月。”

“我看今晚他们已经让他们醒过酒来了。”年轻人说。

“他们是尽了力了。”雷迪说，“我瞧他们能顺顺当当撑到底。这四位眼下是好时光，可往后一辈子，一年四回的领恤日，够他们回味今天的。”

===Original===
“Who are going to take part in the procession?” asked Cleary.

“They’re going to have all the military companies and patriotic societies of these parts,” answered Reddy, “and then the firemen too of course; but they won’t amount to much, for most of them are in the societies, and they’d rather turn out in them.”

“What societies are there?” said Cleary.

“Oh, there’s the Grandsons of the Revolution and the Genuine Grandsons of the Revolution, and the Daughters of Revolutionary Camp-Followers and the Genuine Daughters, and then the Male Descendants of Second Cousins of Heroes, and the Genuine Male Descendants, and the Connections by Marriage of Colonial Tax-Collectors, and then the Genuine Connections, and a lot of others I can’t remember.”

===Chinese===
“都有谁参加游行啊？”克利里问。

“这一带的军事连队和各爱国协会全都要出动。”雷迪答道，“消防队当然也少不了；不过他们出不了什么彩，因为队员大多也入了协会，宁可跟着协会走。”

“都有些什么协会？”克利里问。

“嗐，有革命之孙会，还有正宗革命之孙会；有革命随营者之女会，还有正宗革命随营者之女会；还有英雄远房表亲男性后裔会，和正宗男性后裔会；还有殖民地税吏姻亲会，和正宗姻亲会；另外还有好些个，我一时记不清了。”

===Original===
“The names seem to go in pairs,” said Cleary.

“Well, you see, they always have a fight about something in these military societies, and then they split, and the party that splits away always takes the same name and puts ‘Genuine’ in front of it. That’s the way it is.”

“I suppose these societies do a lot of good, don’t they?” asked Cleary. “These splits and quarrels remind me of the army. They must spread the military spirit among the people.”

“Yes, they do,” said the young man. “It’s what they call esprit de corps. If fighting is military, they fight and no mistake, and the women fight more than the men. I don’t know how many lawsuits they’ve had. Half of them won’t speak to the other half. But they’re all united on one thing, I can tell you, and that is in wanting to put down the Cubapinos.”

===Chinese===
“这些名号看来都是成双成对的。”克利里说。

“这个嘛，您瞧，这些军事协会里头，总要为什么事干上一架，跟着就闹分裂；分裂出去的那一拨，名号照旧不改，只在头里添上‘正宗’两个字。就是这么回事。”

“我猜这些协会想必做了不少好事吧？”克利里问，“这些分家吵架的事倒叫我想起了军队。它们准是把尚武精神散布到民间去了。”

“可不是。”年轻人说，“他们管这个叫‘团队精神’（esprit de corps）。要说打架也算军事，他们打得可真不含糊，女人比男人还能打。官司打了多少场，我都数不清了。这一半人同那一半人连话都不讲。可有一样他们是齐心的，我告诉您——那就是想制服古巴平人。”

===Original===
“That they are,” cried Reddy. “That’s why they call ’em ‘Patriotic Societies.’ It was our ancestors as fought for freedom that they made the societies for. Our ancestors were patriotic and fought for freedom oncet, and now we’re going to be patriotic and stick by the government just like they did.”

“Yes, they fought for freedom, that’s true. And what are the Cubapinos fighting for?” asked the young man.

“Oh, shucks!” cried Reddy. “I ain’t a-going to argher with you. What were we talking about? Oh, yes. We were saying that them societies fight together. They do fight a good deal, that’s a fact, and there’s no end of trouble in our militia battalion too. They all want to be captain, and they don’t get on somehow as well as the fire companies. But still it’s a fine thing to see all this military spirit. I didn’t see a uniform for years, and now you can’t hire a man to dig a ditch who hasn’t got a stripe on one leg of his trousers at any rate. Girls like soldiers, I tell you, and they like pensions too. I’ve just got married myself. My wife is seventeen. Now I’ve drawed my pension for nearly forty years, and she’ll draw it for sixty more if she has any luck; that’ll make over a hundred. That’s something like. Why, if one of these fellows is twenty now and marries a girl of seventeen when he’s ninety, and she lives till she’s ninety, they can keep drawing money for a hundred and fifty years, and no mistake. It’s better than a savings bank. Here they come!”

===Chinese===
“可不齐心嘛！”雷迪嚷道，“要不怎么管它们叫‘爱国协会’呢。这些协会，本来就是为我们那些为自由打过仗的祖宗才立的。我们的祖宗爱国，当年为自由打了一仗；如今咱们也要爱国，也要像他们那样死心塌地跟着政府走。”

“是啊，他们为自由打过仗，这不假。那古巴平人打仗又是为的什么呢？”年轻人问。

“嗐，扯哪儿去了！”雷迪嚷道，“我才不跟你抬这个杠呢。咱们刚才说到哪儿了？哦，对了，说这些协会打架。架嘛，他们倒真打了不少，这是实情；咱们民兵营里头也是别扭事儿没完没了。人人个个都想当队长，不知怎么的就是处不到消防队那么齐心。可话说回来，能看见这满世界的尚武精神，总归是件美事。我有好些年连一身制服都没见过，如今你就是雇个人来挖沟，也雇不到一个裤腿上至少不镶一道杠子的了。姑娘家喜欢大兵，我跟你说，连恤金一块儿喜欢。我自己才刚结婚，我媳妇十七岁。我领恤金领了快四十年了，她要是运气好，还能再领六十年，加起来就是一百多年。这才叫够意思。嗐，这些小伙子里面，要是哪个现在二十岁，九十岁上娶个十七岁的姑娘，姑娘又一直活到九十岁，两口子就能一直领上一百五十年，错不了。这可比储蓄银行还强。瞧，来了！”

===Original===
The procession had formed round the corner at the other end of the main street, and now the band began to play, and the column could be seen advancing. First the band passed with an escort of small boys running along in the gutter on either side. Then came two carriages containing the heroes, two in each. They held themselves stiffly and took off their hats, and no one would have supposed that they had drunk too much if the fact had not been universally understood by the public. Behind them came a line of other carriages in which were seated the magnates of the town, including the officeholders and the prominent business men. They all had that self-important air which is inseparable from such shows and which denotes that the individual is feeling either like a great man or a fool. Then came the militia battalion, a rather shamefaced lot of young men who seemed to be painfully aware that they were not at all real heroes like the soldiers in the carriages, but merely make-believe imitations. The patriotic societies followed, genuine and non-genuine, resplendent in “insignia,” sashes, and badges.

===Chinese===
游行队伍已在大街那一头的拐角处排好，这时乐队奏响了乐，长队渐渐望得见开过来了。打头的是乐队，两侧沿着街边阴沟各有一群小男孩追着跑。随后过来两辆马车，载着那几位英雄，每车两位。他们身子挺得僵直，频频举帽致意；若不是这事早已尽人皆知，谁也不会疑心他们喝多了。他们后面跟着一列马车，坐着城里的达官贵人，有当官的，也有商界的头面人物。个个都带着那股同此类场面须臾不可分的自命不凡的神气——那神气表明，此人此刻自觉不是个大人物，便是个大傻瓜。再后面是民兵营，一群年轻人个个面有愧色，似乎苦于自知同车中那些士兵全然不同，根本不是真英雄，不过是装样子的赝品。接着过来的是各爱国协会，正宗的和非正宗的，佩着“徽记”、绶带和证章，一片光华灿烂。

===Original===
“There’s my wife, she’s a G.C.M.C.T.C.,” said Reddy proudly, pointing out a very plain young woman with gold spectacles. “And here come the Genuine Ancestors of Future Veterans. See that old woman there on the other side? She made all the fuss. You see when anybody wants to get into a society and finds they can’t get in they go off and start another. And some people that hadn’t any tax collectors or connections or anything, they just got up the ‘Ancestors of Future Veterans,’ and everybody in town wanted to get into that. And old Miss Blunt there, she wanted to come in too, and she’s over seventy, and they said she couldn’t be an ancestor nohow, and she said she could and she would, and they voted forty-one to forty against her, and the forty went off and founded the Genuine Ancestors, and they’re twice as big as the others now. Hear ’em applaud?”

===Chinese===
“那一位是我媳妇，她是‘正宗殖民地税吏姻亲会’（G.C.M.C.T.C.）的会员。”雷迪指着一位戴金丝眼镜、相貌十分平常的年轻女人，得意地说，“这边过来的是‘正宗未来老兵之祖先会’。看见对面那个老太太没有？那场风波全是她闹起来的。您知道，谁想进一个协会，眼见进不去，就出去另立一个。有些人家里既没出过税吏，也攀不上什么姻亲，什么都没有，他们就干脆发起了一个‘未来老兵之祖先会’，全城的人都想往里进。那边那位布伦特老小姐（Miss Blunt），她也想进，她都七十开外了，人家说她横竖算不得一位祖先，她偏说算得，还非进不可。表决下来，四十一票对四十票，把她否了；那四十个人便出去另立了‘正宗祖先会’，如今人比原先那个会多出一倍。听见没有，这掌声？”

===Original===
The old lady walked along with a martial tread, and was loudly cheered as she passed.

“Now we’d better get into the church if we want seats,” said the young man, and Cleary followed him, leaving the ancient warrior behind. The church was very crowded and very hot, and Cleary had to sit on a step of the platform, but it was an exhibition of patriotism worth beholding. The band played with great gusto, and the whole audience was at the highest pitch of excitement. The chairman made an address, and Josh Thatcher responded in a few words for himself and his three companions. Then flowers were presented to them, and a little girl recited the “Charge of the Light Brigade,” but the main feature of the program was the oration of Dr. Taylor, the pastor of the church. He was famed as an orator not only in his denomination and in the county but in the National Order of Total Abstinence, of which he was a leading light. In his address he welcomed the four heroes back to their hearths and firesides. He thanked them for having conquered so many lands and spread the blessings of civilization and Christianity to the ends of the earth.

===Chinese===
老太太迈着军人的步伐走在队伍里，一路过去，喝彩声不断。

“咱们要想有座位坐，就趁早进教堂吧。”年轻人说。克利里随着他进去，把那员老将留在了身后。教堂里拥挤不堪，又十分闷热，克利里只好在讲台的踏级上坐了下来；不过这是一场值得一看的爱国主义展览。乐队奏得酣畅起劲，满场听众的兴奋达到了极点。主席先生致了辞，乔什·撒切尔（Josh Thatcher）代表自己和三位同伴简短答谢了几句。接着有人向他们献了花，一个小姑娘朗诵了《轻骑兵的冲锋》（“Charge of the Light Brigade”）；不过节目的重头戏，是本堂牧师泰勒博士（Dr. Taylor）的演说。他是鼎鼎大名的演说家——不仅在本教派、在本县有名，在全国戒酒会（National Order of Total Abstinence）里也是数一数二的人物，那组织的台柱之一。他在演说里欢迎四位英雄回到各自的炉灶火塘边，感谢他们征服了那么多的国土，把文明与基督教的恩泽一路播撒到了地角天涯。

===Original===
“We have been told, my friends, by wicked and unpatriotic scoffers, that these wars have stirred up the passions of our people, that there are more lynchings and deeds of violence than ever before, and that negro soldiers returning from the war have shot down citizens from car-windows. I have even been told that its effect is to be seen in the attempts of worthy citizens, including a distinguished judge, to have the whipping-post reestablished in our midst. I can only say for myself that such traitors and traducers should be the first victims of the whipping-post. (Cheers.) So far from crime having increased since the departure of these young heroes, I can testify that there has been a marked decrease in our community. Since they left, not a single barn has been burned, not a chicken stolen. My friend, Mrs. Crane, informs me that she keeps more chickens than ever before, and that she has not missed one in over a year. I am also told that during the absence of these young men the amount of liquor drunk in our town has sensibly diminished. The war then has been a blessing to us and to our nation.”

===Chinese===
“我的朋友们，有邪恶而不爱国的冷嘲之徒对我们说，这几场战争撩动了我等百姓的血气，说私刑和种种暴行比以往任何时候都多，说打了仗回来的黑人士兵从车窗里开枪射杀公民。甚至还有人告诉我，其影响之所及，竟教一些正派的公民——其中还有一位显赫的法官——力图在我们中间重立鞭刑柱。对这种种，我本人只想说一句：这样的卖国贼和诽谤之徒，理应头一个送上鞭刑柱受刑。（掌声）自这几位年轻英雄出征以来，非但犯罪没有增加，我敢作证，我们这个社区反倒明显减少了。自他们走后，没有一间谷仓被烧掉，没有一只鸡被偷走。我的朋友克兰太太（Mrs. Crane）告诉我，她养的鸡比以往任何时候都多，一年多来一只也不曾短少。我还听说，这几位年轻人不在的这段日子里，我们镇上的酒类消耗量已经明显下降。可见这场战争，于我们、于我们的国家，都是一场福佑。”

===Original===
During these remarks Josh Thatcher, who was sitting in the front row, gave sundry digs in the ribs to his cousin Tom, and they both laughed aloud.

“We welcome our heroes back,” continued the orator. “We open our arms to them. All that we have is theirs. We applaud their manly courage and Christian self-sacrifice. We shall never, never forget their services, and we shall recite their noble deeds to our children and to our children’s children.”

===Chinese===
这番话讲着的时候，坐在前排的乔什·撒切尔不住用胳膊肘捅他的堂弟汤姆（Tom），两人放声大笑。

“我们欢迎我们的英雄归来。”演说家继续说道，“我们向他们张开双臂。我们所有的一切都属于他们。我们为他们男子汉的气概和基督徒式的自我牺牲鼓掌。我们永远、永远不会忘记他们的功绩，我们要把他们的高尚行迹，讲给我们的儿女听，讲给我们儿女的儿女听。”

===Original===
The meeting broke up with three cheers and a tiger for each of the four heroes. For an hour later the crowds stood in the street talking over the great events of the day, each of the young veterans forming the center of an admiring group, Tom Thatcher being surrounded by a bevy of pretty girls who seemed to find nothing objectionable in his pimpled face and hoarse voice. Cleary stood for a long time watching them and talking with the insurance man.

“It’s their night,” said the latter, “but it won’t last long. We know them too well. When the barns begin to burn again, folks’ll all know what it means. I wish they’d keep a war going a long way off forever for these fellows. It would be a good riddance. And that’s all talk of old Taylor’s anyway. He won’t take them to his heart, not by a great deal. I heard Dave Black ask him for a job today, and he wants a man too, and he said, ‘What﻿—an ex-soldier? Not much!’ The words were out of his mouth before he knew what he’d said. He’s a slick one.”

===Chinese===
散会时，人们对四位英雄每一位都是三声欢呼，外加一声“虎吼”。此后一个来钟头，人群还站在街上谈论当天的大事，几位年轻的老兵个个成了一群崇拜者环拱的中心；汤姆·撒切尔身边更是围了一群漂亮姑娘，她们对他那张满是粉刺的脸和那副嘶哑的嗓子，似乎挑不出半点可嫌的地方。克利里站在那里看了半天，一边同那位保险代理说话。

“今晚是他们的一夜。”后者说，“可长不了。他们的底细，我们太清楚了。等谷仓重新烧起来，大家就都知道是怎么回事了。我倒巴不得给这几位在老远的地方永远打一场仗，那才叫眼不见心不烦。反正老泰勒那一套全是空口白话。他才不会把他们揣进心尖上呢，差得远。今天我就听见戴夫·布莱克（Dave Black）找他讨差事，他手头也正要人，可他说：‘什么——退伍大兵？可不行！’话出了口，他才回过神来自己说了什么。这老家伙，滑得很。”

===Original===
When Cleary returned to Mr. Jinks’ house, he found Sam much worse, and the gravest fears were entertained as to his recovery. In the morning he was a little easier, and Cleary was able to have a little talk with him before he left. Sam had been told by the doctor that his condition was serious, and he had no desire to get well.

“You must brace up, old man,” said Cleary cheerily. “I’ll come back in a few days and we’ll lay out our plans for the future. You’re the finest soldier that ever lived, and I haven’t done with you yet.”

“Don’t say that, don’t say that!” cried Sam. “I’m no soldier at all. I wanted to be a perfect soldier, and I can’t. It’s that that’s breaking my heart. I don’t mind the nomination for President nor anything else in comparison. My poor wife! Why did I let her marry a coward like me? I can’t tell you now, but if I’m alive when you come here again I’ll tell you all.”

“Nonsense, old man,” said Cleary. “You’ve got the fever on you again. It’s in your blood. When it gets out, you’ll be all right.”

===Chinese===
克利里回到金克斯先生府上，只见萨姆的病势大为沉重，人们对他能否康复，已抱着最坏的忧虑。到了早晨，他稍稍平顺了些，克利里临走之前得以同他短短谈了几句。医生已经告诉萨姆，他的病情危重，而他已无意求活。

“你得振作起来，老伙计。”克利里高高兴兴地宽慰道，“过几天我就回来，咱们把今后的计划好好筹划筹划。你是天下有生以来最出色的士兵，我跟你还没完呢。”

“别说这话，别说这话！”萨姆嚷道，“我根本算不上士兵。我本想做一个完美的士兵，可我做不到。伤我心的正是这个。比起它来，总统提名也好，别的什么也好，我都满不在乎。我可怜的妻子！我怎么会让她嫁给我这样一个懦夫？这话眼下还不能对你讲，可等你下回来，我要还活着，我就把一切都告诉你。”

“胡说，老伙计。”克利里说，“你的热病又上来了，病根在血里。等它退出去，你就没事了。”

===Original===
It was with tears in his eyes that Cleary bade his friend goodbye, for he could see that he was a very sick man. It was impossible, however, for him to remain longer, and as Sam’s wife and cousin were there to nurse him, and his father and mother had been telegraphed for, he felt that there was no necessity for him to remain.

After the lapse of three weeks Cleary received the sad news that Sam had shown unmistakable signs of insanity and had been removed to an insane asylum. His father wrote that while his insanity was of a mild form, the doctors thought it best for him to be placed in an institution where he could receive the most scientific treatment. Six months later Cleary, who was now one of the editors of the *Lyre*, went on a sad pilgrimage to see his friend. The asylum was several hours away from the metropolis beyond East Point, and was none other than the great building which they had described to the chief of the Moritos. Cleary took a carriage at the station and drove to his destination, and at last arrived at the huge edifice in the midst of its wide domain. He went into the reception-room and explained his errand. After a while a young doctor came to him, and told him that he could have an interview with Captain Jinks at once, and offered to act as his guide. It was a long walk through corridors and passages and up winding stairs to Sam’s apartment, and Cleary questioned the doctor as they went.

===Chinese===
克利里是含着眼泪同朋友道别的，因为他看得出，这人已经病得很重。然而他不可能再多留了；萨姆的妻子和一位堂兄弟都在跟前看护，他的父母也已拍电报去接，他觉得自己没有再守着的必要。

三个星期过去，克利里接到那个不幸的消息：萨姆已显出不容置疑的疯癫迹象，被送进了疯人院。他父亲写信说，他的疯病虽属轻症，医生们还是认为，把他安置在一所能施以最科学治疗的机构里为好。半年之后，此时已是《里拉报》编辑之一的克利里，踏上一次黯然的旅程，去探望他的朋友。那所疯人院离大都有好几个钟头的路程，在过了东点更远处，不是别的，正是他们当年向莫里托人首领描述过的那座大房子。克利里在车站雇了一辆马车，驱车前往目的地，终于到了那片广阔地界中央的庞大楼馆跟前。他走进接待室，说明了来意。过了一会儿，一位年轻医生来到他跟前，告诉他可以立刻会见金克斯上尉，并主动提出为他引路。到萨姆的房间去要走很长一段路，穿过一条条走廊和过道，再登上盘旋的楼梯，克利里一路向医生问这问那。

===Original===
“Captain Jinks is a dear fellow,” said the doctor in response to his inquiries. “We are all fond of him. At first he was a little intractable and denied our right to direct him, but now that we’ve got it all down on a military basis, he will do anything we tell him. I believe he would walk out of the window if I ordered him too. But I have to put on a military coat to make him obey. We keep one on purpose. As soon as he sees it on anybody he’s as obedient as a child. He’s such a perfect gentleman, too. It’s a very sad case. Here’s his room.”

The doctor knocked.

“Who goes there?” cried a husky voice, which Cleary hardly recognized as Sam’s.

“A friend,” answered the doctor.

“Advance, friend, and give the countersign,” said the same voice.

“Old Gory!” cried the doctor, with most unmilitary emphasis, and he opened the door and they entered.

===Chinese===
“金克斯上尉是个可爱的人。”医生回答他的询问说，“我们大家都喜欢他。起初他有点儿倔，不认我们有权指挥他，可如今我们把这里一概按军事规矩办下来，他叫干什么就干什么。我相信，只要我下命令，他连窗户都肯走出去。不过我得穿上一件军装，他才肯服从。我们特为备着一件。他一见谁穿上它，就乖得跟孩子一样。他还是那么一位地道的绅士。这个病例实在可悲。到了，这就是他的房间。”

医生敲了敲门。

“什么人？”一个嘶哑的声音喝道，克利里几乎听不出这是萨姆的声音。

“朋友。”医生答道。

“朋友，上前，对口令。”还是那个声音说。

“老光荣！”（Old Gory，星条旗昵称“老荣耀”的讹音）医生喊道，那腔调全无半点军人气概。他推开门，两人走了进去。

===Original===
Cleary saw what seemed to be the shadow of Sam, pale, haggard, and emaciated, sitting in a shabby undress uniform before a large deal table. Upon the table was a most elaborate arrangement of books and blocks of wood, apparently representing fortifications, which were manned by a dilapidated set of lead soldiers﻿—the earliest treasures of Sam’s boyhood, which had been sent to him from home at his request. Sam did not lift his eyes from the table, and moved the men about with his hand as if he were playing a game of chess.

“Here is a friend of yours to see you, Captain,” said the doctor.

Sam slowly raised his head and looked at Cleary for some time without recognizing him. Gradually a faint smile made its appearance.

“I know you,” he said in the same strained voice. “I know you. You’re﻿—”

“Cleary,” said Cleary.

“Cleary? Cleary? Let me see. Why, to be sure, you’re Cleary.” And he rose from his chair unsteadily and took the hand that Cleary offered him.

===Chinese===
克利里看见的，仿佛是萨姆的一道影子：苍白、枯槁、形销骨立，穿一身寒酸的军便服，坐在一张大松木桌前。桌上摆着一套用书本和木块搭成的煞费经营的阵势，显然代表各处要塞，由一套破破烂烂的铅制士兵把守——那是萨姆童年最早的珍藏，应他自己的请求从家里寄了来。萨姆眼睛不离桌面，用手挪动着那些小人，仿佛在对弈一盘棋。

“上尉，您的一位朋友来看您了。”医生说。

萨姆缓缓抬起头，朝克利里望了好一阵，没有认出他来。渐渐地，一丝淡淡的笑意浮上脸来。

“我认得你。”他还是用那副发紧的嗓音说，“我认得你。你是——”

“克利里。”克利里说。

“克利里？克利里？我想想。哎呀，可不是，你是克利里。”他从椅子上摇摇晃晃地站起来，握住了克利里伸出的手。

===Original===
“How are you, old man? I’m so glad to see you again,” said Cleary.

“And so am I,” said Sam, who now seemed to be almost his old self again. “Sit down.”

Cleary drew up a chair to the table, while the doctor retired and shut the door.

“How are you getting on?” said Cleary. “You’re going to get well soon, aren’t you?”

“I am well now,” said Sam. “I was awfully ill, I know that, but it all came from my mind. I think I told you that. My heart was breaking because I couldn’t be a perfect soldier. I had to face the question and grapple with it. It was an awful experience; I can’t bear to speak of it or even think of it. But I won. I’m a perfect soldier now! I can do anything with my men here, and I will obey any order I receive, I don’t care what it is.”

===Chinese===
“你好啊，老伙计？又见着你了，我真高兴。”克利里说。

“我也高兴。”萨姆说，这时他似乎几乎又是从前的他了。“请坐。”

克利里把一把椅子拉到桌前，医生退了出去，随手带上了门。

“你近来怎么样？”克利里说，“你快好了吧？”

“我现在好了。”萨姆说，“我病得厉害，这我知道，可那全是打我心上来的。我想我同你说过的。我的心一直碎着，因为我做不成一个完美的士兵。我不得不迎面对付这个问题，同它搏斗。那是一段可怕的经历；我不忍说它，连想也不忍想。可是我赢了。我现在是个完美的士兵了！我在这儿指挥我这些兵，要怎样就怎样；我接到的命令，无论什么我都服从，不管它是什么命令。”

===Original===
As he spoke of his experience a pained expression came over his face, but he looked proud and almost happy when he announced the result of the conflict.

“They say I’m a lunatic, I know they do,” he continued, looking round to see that no one else was present, and lowering his voice to a whisper. “They say I’m a lunatic, but I’m not. When they say I’m a lunatic they mean I’m a perfect soldier﻿—a complete soldier. And they call those fine fellows lead soldiers! Lunatics and lead soldiers indeed! Well, suppose we are! I tell you an army of lead soldiers with a lunatic at the head would be the best army in the world. We do what we’re told, and we’re not afraid of anything.”

===Chinese===
他讲起自己那段经历时，一脸痛楚掠过面容；可当他宣布这场搏斗的结果时，神色却是骄傲的，几乎是快活的。

“他们说我是个疯子，我知道他们这么说。”他接着讲道，一边回头张望，见再没有旁人在场，便把嗓音压成耳语，“他们说我是个疯子，可我不是。他们说我疯，意思是说我是个完美的士兵——一个十全的士兵。他们还管这些好汉子叫铅兵呢！疯子和铅兵，说得多好！好，就算是吧！我告诉你，一支铅兵的军队，由一个疯子统带，就是天底下最好的军队。叫我们做什么我们就做什么，而且我们什么都不怕。”

===Original===
Sam stopped talking at this juncture and went on for some time in silence maneuvering his troops. Finally he picked up the colonel with the white plume, and a ray of light from the afternoon sun fell upon it, and he held it before him, gazing upon it entranced. The door opened, and the doctor entered.

“I fear you must go now, Mr. Cleary. He can’t stand much excitement. He’s quiet now. Just come out with me without saying anything,” and Cleary followed him out of the room, while Sam sat motionless with his eyes fixed on his talisman.

===Chinese===
说到这里，萨姆住了口，好一阵只顾默默调度他的部队。末了，他拾起那个插白翎的上校，一道午后的阳光正落在翎子上；他把它举在眼前，凝望着它，出了神。门开了，医生走进来。

“恐怕您得走了，克利里先生。他受不得一点激动。他这会儿安静。请您什么也别说，跟我出来吧。”克利里随他走出房间，萨姆坐在那里一动不动，眼睛牢牢盯着他的护符。

===Original===
“He sits like that for hours,” said the doctor. “It’s a kind of hypnotism, I think, which we don’t quite understand yet. I am writing up the case for *The Medical Gazette*. It’s a peculiar kind of insanity, this preoccupation with uniforms and soldiers, and the readiness to do anything a man in regimentals tells him to.”

“It’s rather more common, perhaps, out of asylums than in them,” muttered Cleary, but the doctor did not hear him. “Do you think he will ever recover, doctor?” he continued.

The doctor shook his head ominously.

“And will he live to old age in this condition?”

“He might, if there were nothing else the matter with him, but there is, and perhaps it’s a fortunate thing. He’s got a new disease called filariasis, a sort of low fever that he picked up in the Cubapines or Porsslania. There’s a good deal of it among the soldiers who have come back. We have a lot of lunatics from the army here and several of them have this new fever too. It wouldn’t kill him alone, either, but the two things together will surely carry him off. He will hardly live another half-year.”

===Chinese===
“他常常这样一坐好几个钟头。”医生说，“我想这是一种催眠术，我们还不十分了然。我正把这个病例整理成文，投给《医学公报》（The Medical Gazette）。这是一种特别的疯病：一心扑在军装和士兵上头，穿军装的人叫他做什么他就做什么。”

“这种病，兴许疯人院外头比院里还多见些。”克利里喃喃道，医生却没有听见。“大夫，您看他还有复原的希望吗？”他又问。

医生神色不祥地摇了摇头。

“那他这样能活到老年吗？”

“要是他没别的毛病，倒也说不定能。可他有，而这也许是件幸事。他得了一种叫丝虫病的新病，一种低热，是在古巴平还是波斯兰尼亚染上的。回来的兵里头害这病的不少。我们这儿收着好些军队里来的疯子，他们当中有几个也害着这种新热病。单这一样也弄不死他，可两样加在一起，准能要他的命。他多半活不过半年了。”

===Original===
“I suppose his family is looking out for him?” said Cleary.

“His mother visits him pretty regularly, and his father comes sometimes,” said the doctor, “but I think his wife has only been here twice. And she’s living at East Point, too, only an hour or two away. She’s a born flirt, and I think she’s tired of him. I’m told that one of this year’s graduates there, a fellow named Saunders, is paying attention to her, and when the poor captain dies, I doubt if she remains long a widow.”

===Chinese===
“我想，他家里总有人照应着他吧？”克利里说。

“他母亲来得很勤，父亲有时也来。”医生说，“不过他媳妇，我想统共只来过两回。何况她就住在东点，离这儿不过一两个钟头的路。她是个天生卖弄风情的女子，我看她是把他腻烦透了。听说东点今年毕业的一个学员，一个姓桑德斯（Saunders）的小子，正对她大献殷勤；等可怜的上尉一死，我看她的寡妇也做不长久。”

===Original===
“Then I suppose there is nothing I can do for the dear old chap?” asked Cleary, with tears in his eyes, as he took his leave of the doctor at the door of the building.

“Nothing at all, my dear sir. He has everything he wants, and in fact he wants nothing but his lead soldiers. He won’t even let us give him a new set of them. And he has all the liberty he wants on the grounds here, and he can walk or even take a drive if he wishes to, for he is perfectly harmless.”

“Perfectly harmless!” repeated Cleary to himself, as he got into his carriage. “What an idea! A perfectly harmless soldier!”

===Chinese===
“那么，这位亲爱的老伙计，我是没有什么可为他做的了？”克利里在楼门口向医生告辞时，眼里含着泪问道。

“一点也没有了，亲爱的先生。他要的东西样样都有，其实除了他的铅兵，他什么也不要。连换一套新的铅兵，他都不肯。在这片园地里，他愿意怎样自由都可以，想散步就散步，想坐车兜风也随他，因为他完全不伤人。”

“完全不伤人！”克利里一面登车，一面自言自语地重复道，“好一个想法！一个完全不伤人的士兵！”

## XII The Great White Temple / 十二 大白庙

===Original===

On the following morning the two friends started on their journey up the river toward the Imperial City. They went on a barge filled with soldiers, some of them their own troops who had arrived earlier the same morning. The barge was drawn by ropes pulled by natives, who walked and ran along the banks of the river. It was a day of ever-increasing horrors. All the desolation which they had remarked the day previous was reproduced and accentuated, and as they were so much nearer to the bank, and occasionally took walks on shore, they saw it all more clearly. Sam was much interested in the foreign troops. Their uniforms looked strange and uncouth.

“What funny pillboxes those are that those Anglian soldiers have stuck to the side of their heads,” he said, pointing to two men at Gin-Sin before they set sail.

“Yes,” answered Cleary. “They’ll put on their helmets when the sun gets higher. They do look queer, though. Perhaps they think our fellows look queer too.”

“I never thought of that,” said Sam. “Perhaps they do,” and he looked at his fellow-countrymen who were preparing to embark, endeavoring to judge of their appearance as if he had never seen them before. He scrutinized carefully their slouch hats creased in four quarters, their loose, dark-blue jackets, generally unbuttoned, and their easygoing movements.

“Perhaps they do look queer,” he said at last. “I never thought of that.”

===Chinese===

次日清晨，两位朋友动身溯江而上，往京城去。他们登上一艘满载士兵的驳船，其中一部分是他们自己的部队，是当天早晨先期抵达的。驳船靠纤绳拖曳，拉纤的是土人，沿着河岸又走又跑。这一天，种种惨状愈演愈烈。头一天他们已见过的那满目荒凉，此刻重现眼前，而且变本加厉；由于这回离河岸近得多，又不时上岸走走，一切便看得更加分明。萨姆对外国军队大感兴趣。他们的军装瞧着古怪，粗笨失体。

“瞧那些安格利亚（Anglian）士兵歪贴在脑袋一边的小圆盒，多滑稽啊。”开船之前，他在金辛指着两个士兵说道。

“是啊，”克利里答道，“等太阳升高些，他们就会戴上头盔了。不过，他们那样子确实够怪的。兴许他们看咱们的人也觉得怪呢。”

“这我倒从没想到过。”萨姆说，“兴许真是呢。”他望着正准备登船的同胞，努力装作平生头一回见他们，端详起他们的模样来。他细细打量他们那四瓣折痕的宽边软帽、一般都敞着怀的藏青色宽松上衣，还有那副散漫随和的做派。

“他们看起来，兴许真有点怪。”他末了说道，“这话我从前可想都没想过。”

===Original===

The river was more full of corpses than ever, and there were many to be seen on the shore, all of them of natives. Children were playing and bathing in the shallows, oblivious of the dead around them. Dogs prowled about, sleek and contented, and usually sniffing only at the cadavers, for their appetites were already sated. At one place they saw a father and son lying hand in hand where they had been shot while imploring mercy. A dog was quietly eating the leg of the boy. The natives who pulled the boat along with great difficulty under the hot sun were drawn from all classes, some of them coolies accustomed to hard work, others evidently of the leisure classes who could hardly keep up with the rest. Soldiers were acting as taskmasters, and they whipped the men who did not pull with sufficient strength. Now and then a man would try to escape by running, but such deserters were invariably brought down by a bullet in the back. More than once one of the men would fall as they waded along, and be swept off by the current. None of them seemed to know how to swim, but no one paid any attention to their fate. Parties were sent out to bring in other natives to take the place of those who gave out. One of the men thus brought in was paralyzed on one side and carried a crutch. The soldiers made sport of him, snatched the crutch from him, and made him pull as best he could with the rest. Sam, Cleary, and an Anglian officer who had served through the whole war took a long walk together back from the river during the halt at noon. They entered a deserted house, with gables and a tiled roof, which by chance had not been burned. The house had been looted, and such of its contents as were too large to carry away were lying broken to bits about the floor. A nasty smell came from an inner room, and they looked in and saw the whole family﻿—father, mother, and three daughters﻿—lying dead in a row on the floor. A bloody knife was in the hand of the man.

===Chinese===

江里的尸体比先前更多了，岸上也处处可见，全是土人。孩子们在浅水里嬉戏洗浴，对周遭的死人浑然不觉。狗四处游荡，一个个毛色油亮、心满意足，对尸首通常只嗅上一嗅——肚子早已填饱了。有一处，他们看见父子俩手牵着手倒在那里，是哀求饶命时被枪杀的。一条狗正不紧不慢地啃着那男孩的腿。顶着烈日艰难拉纤的土人来自各个阶层：有的是干惯了重活的苦力，有的显然是有闲阶级，几乎跟不上趟。士兵们充当监工，谁拉得不够卖力就抽谁。间或有人撒腿想逃，这样的逃亡者无一例外都被一颗子弹从背后撂倒。纤夫们蹚水前行，不止一次有人失足跌倒，被激流卷走。他们看上去没有一个会水的，可谁也不理会他们的死活。一队队士兵被派出去，抓来别的土人，顶替那些力竭倒下的。有个这样被抓来的人半身不遂，拄着一副拐。士兵们拿他取乐，夺走他的拐杖，逼他同大伙儿一起竭力拉纤。中午停船的当口，萨姆、克利里和一位打满了全场战事的安格利亚军官一起，离船往岸上远处散步。他们走进一所幸免于火的空屋，带山墙，铺瓦顶。屋子已遭洗劫，那些大得搬不走的家什，都摔碎在地上，狼藉一片。里屋透出一股恶臭，他们探头看去，只见一家人——父亲、母亲和三个女儿——一字排开，死在地板上。那男人手里握着一把血淋淋的刀。

===Original===

“They probably committed suicide when they saw the soldiers coming,” said the Anglian, whose name was Major Brown. “They often do that, and they do quite right. When they don’t, the soldiers, and even the officers sometimes, do what they will with the women and then bayonet them afterward. Our people draw the line at that, and so do yours.”

“We certainly conduct war most humanely,” said Sam.

They heard a groan from another room, and opening the door saw an old woman lying in a pool of blood, quite unconscious.

“I’ll put her out of her misery,” said the major, and he drew his revolver and shot her through the head.

===Chinese===

“大概是瞧见士兵们来了，就自尽了。”那位安格利亚人说——他名叫布朗少校（Major Brown）。“他们常这么干，而且干得对。不这么干的话，士兵们、有时连军官们，就会对女人为所欲为，事后再把她们挑死在刺刀上。这种事我们的人尚知有所不为，你们的人也一样。”

“我们打起仗来，确实再人道不过了。”萨姆说。

他们听见另一间屋里发出一声呻吟，推开门，只见一个老妇人躺在一摊血泊里，全然不省人事。

“我来让她少受点罪。”少校说着，拔出左轮枪，一枪打穿了她的头。

===Original===

The journey was a very slow one and occupied three days, although the natives were kept at work as long as they could stand it, on one day actually tugging at the ropes for twenty-one hours. At last, however, the Imperial City was reached, and our two travelers disembarked and, taking a donkey-cart, gave directions to carry them to the quarter assigned to their own army. Here as everywhere desolation reigned. A string of laden camels showed, however, that trade was beginning to reassert itself. They drove past miles of burned houses, through the massive city walls and beyond, until they saw the welcome signs of a camp over which Old Gory waved supreme. Sam was received with much cordiality by the commandant, General Taffy, and assigned to the command of the 27th Volunteer Infantry. The general was a man well known throughout the army for his courage and ability, but notwithstanding this Sam took a strong prejudice against him, for he seemed to be halfhearted in his work and to disapprove of the prevailing policy of pacification by fire and sword. Sam ascribed this feebleness to the fact that he had been originally appointed to the army from civil life, and that he had not enjoyed the benefits of an East Point education.

===Chinese===

旅途走得极慢，足足花去三天，尽管土人只要还撑得住就被逼着干活，有一天竟拉了二十一个钟头的纤。京城终于到了，我们的两位旅人下了船，雇上一辆驴车，吩咐拉到划给他们自己军队的防区。这里同别处一样，满目疮痍。不过，一长列驮着货物的骆驼表明，买卖已开始重整旗鼓。他们驶过绵延数英里的焚毁房屋，穿过厚重的城墙再往前，终于望见一座军营那叫人亲切的景象——老光荣旗在上空至高无上地飘着。司令官塔菲将军（General Taffy）热诚地接待了萨姆，并任命他统带第二十七志愿步兵团。这位将军的骁勇与才干在全军素负盛名；尽管如此，萨姆却对他生了强烈的偏见，因为将军办起差事来似乎有气无力，而且不赞成眼下以火与剑推行绥靖的普遍方针。萨姆把这种软弱归因于两点：将军原是从平民中被任命进军界的，不曾享过东点教育的恩泽。

===Original===

As soon as Sam was installed in his new quarters, in the colonel’s tent of his regiment, he started out with Cleary to see the great city and examine the scene of the late siege. They found the Jap quarter the most populous. The inhabitants who had fled had returned, and the streets were taking on their normal aspect. Near the boundary of this district they saw a house with a placard in the Jap language, and asked an Anglian soldier who was passing what it meant.

“That’s one of the Jap placards to show that the natives who live there are good people who have given no offense,” said he.

“Let’s go in and pay them a call,” said Cleary.

They entered, and passing into a back room found a woman nursing a man who had evidently been recently shot in the side. She shrank from them with terror as they entered, and made no answer to their request for information. As they passed out they met a young native coming in, and they asked him what it meant.

===Chinese===

萨姆一安顿进本团上校住的新营帐，便同克利里出发去游览这座大都，察看不久前围城战的旧迹。他们发现日本人聚居的那一区人烟最稠密。逃难回来的居民已陆续还乡，街市正恢复旧观。在该区边界附近，他们看见一所房子上贴着一张日文告示，便问一个路过的安格利亚士兵那是什么意思。

“那是一种日本人的告示，标明住在里头的土人是安分良民，没有开罪过人。”他答道。

“咱们进去拜访拜访他们吧。”克利里说。

他们进了屋，穿过前屋来到后间，见一个女人正在看护一个男人，那人腰侧显然刚挨了枪。他们一进来，那女人便吓得直往后缩；他们打听缘由，她一言不答。往外走时，他们碰见一个年轻土人正往里来，便问他这是怎么回事。

===Original===

“Some Frank soldiers shot him because he could not give them money. It had all been stolen already,” said the lad in pigeon English.

“But the placard says they are loyal people,” said Cleary.

“What difference does that make to them?” was the reply.

===Chinese===

“几个法兰克（Frank）兵开枪打的他，因为他拿不出钱来。钱早叫人抢光了。”小伙子用洋泾浜英语说。

“可是告示上明明说，他们是归顺的良民呀。”克利里说。

“那对他们来说算得了什么？”对方答道。

===Original===

Farther on in a lonely part of the town they heard cries issuing from the upper window of a house. They were the cries of women, mingled with oaths of men in the Frank language. Suddenly two women jumped out of the window, one after the other, and fell in a bruised mass in the street. Sam and Cleary approached them and saw that they had received a mortal hurt. They were ladies, handsomely dressed. The first impulse of Sam and Cleary was to take charge of them, but seeing two natives approach, they called their attention to the case and walked away.

“I suppose it’s best not to get mixed up with the affairs of the other armies,” said Sam.

The quarter assigned to the Tutonians they were surprised to find quite deserted by the inhabitants.

“I tell you, those Tutonians know their business,” said Sam. “They won’t stand any fooling. Just see how they have established peace! We have a lot to learn from them.”

===Chinese===

再往前去，在城内一处人迹稀少的地方，他们听见一所房子的楼上窗口传出叫喊。那是女人的哭喊，夹杂着男人用法兰克话骂出的脏话。忽然，两个女人一先一后从窗口跳了下来，摔在街上，结成一团血肉模糊。萨姆和克利里走近前去，看出她们已受了致命的重伤。她们是大家闺秀，衣着华贵。萨姆和克利里的第一个念头是亲自照管她们，但见两个土人走近，便把这桩案子指给那两个土人留意，自顾走开了。

“我想，还是别搅进别国军队的事情里去为好。”萨姆说。

划给图托尼亚人的那一区，居民竟逃得一所不剩，这倒叫他们诧异。

“我跟你说，那些图托尼亚人办事真在行。”萨姆说，“他们可容不得半点胡闹。瞧瞧人家是怎么建立太平的！咱们要向人家学的还多着呢。”

===Original===

They saw a crowd collected in one place.

“What is it?” asked Sam of a soldier.

“They’re going to shoot thirty of these damned coolies for jostling soldiers in the street,” he answered.

Sam regretted that they had no time to wait and see the execution.

===Chinese===

他们看见一处聚着一群人。

“那是怎么回事？”萨姆问一个士兵。

“要枪毙三十个这帮该死的苦力，谁叫他们在街上撞了当兵的。”他答道。

萨姆很遗憾，没有工夫留下来看看行刑。

===Original===

As they reentered their own quarter they saw a number of carts loaded down with all sorts of valuable household effects driven along. They asked one of the native drivers what they were doing, and he replied in pigeon English that they were collecting loot for the Rev. Dr. Amen. Farther on some of their own soldiers were conducting an auction of handsome vases and carved ornaments. Sam watched the sale for a few minutes, and bought in one or two beautiful objects for a song for Marian.

“Where did they get all this stuff?” he asked of a lieutenant.

“Oh, anywhere. Some of it from the houses of foreign residents even. But we don’t understand the game as well as old Amen. He’s a corker. He’s grabbed the house of one of his old native enemies here, an awfully rich chap, and sold him out, and now he’s got his converts cleaning out a whole ward. He’s collected a big fine for every convert killed and so much extra for every dollar stolen, and he’s going to use it all for the propagation of the Gospel. He’s as good as a Tutonian, he is.”

“I’m glad we have such a man to represent our faith,” said Sam.

“He’s pretty hard on General Taffy, though,” said the lieutenant. “He says we ought to have the Tutonian mailed fist. Taffy is much too soft, he thinks.”

Sam bit his lips. He could not criticize his superior officer before a subaltern, but he was tempted to.

===Chinese===

重新走回自己防区时，他们看见一队大车满载着各式值钱的家具摆设驶过。他们问一个赶车的土人在干什么，那人用洋泾浜英语答道，是在替阿门牧师博士（Rev. Dr. Amen）收集战利品。再往前，他们自己部队的一些士兵正主持着一场拍卖，卖的是漂亮的花瓶和雕花的摆件。萨姆看了几分钟热闹，捡了个大便宜，买下一两件漂亮物件，预备送给玛丽安。

“这些东西都是从哪儿弄来的？”他问一名中尉。

“哦，哪儿都有。有些还是从外国侨民家里弄来的呢。不过论玩这一手，咱们谁也比不上老阿门。那家伙是真有两下子。他把这儿一个土人老冤家的宅子霸占了——那家伙阔得吓人——连人带产变卖个精光，如今又带着他的教民，把整整一个城区搜刮一空。每死一个教徒，他收一大笔罚金；每被偷一块钱，再加收若干。他打算把这笔钱统统用于传播福音。他简直顶得上一个图托尼亚人了，真格的。”

“我们信仰的阵营里有这么一号人物代表，我很高兴。”萨姆说。

“不过他对塔菲将军可够狠的。”中尉说，“他说咱们就该有图托尼亚人那只铁腕。照他看，塔菲实在太软了。”

萨姆咬了咬嘴唇。当着下级军官的面，他不好非议自己的长官，可他实在有点忍不住。

===Original===

On reaching headquarters Sam found that he was to take charge of a punitive expedition in the North, whose chief object was to be the destruction of native temples, for the purpose of giving the inhabitants a lesson. He was to have command of his own regiment, two companies of cavalry, and a field-battery. They were to set out in two days. He spent the intermediate time in completing the preparations, which had been well under way before his arrival, and in studying the map. No one knew how much opposition he might expect.

===Chinese===

一到司令部，萨姆便得知自己将统率一次北方的惩罚性征讨，其主要目标是捣毁土人的庙宇，好教训教训当地居民。他将指挥自己的团，外加两个骑兵连和一支野战炮队。队伍定于两天后开拔。他把中间这段时光用来完成各项筹备——这些在他到任之前便已着手进行——并用来研究地图。谁也说不准他会遇到多大的抵抗。

===Original===

It was early in the morning on a hot summer day that the expedition left the Capital. Sam was mounted on a fine bay stallion, and felt that he was entirely in his element.

“What camp is that over there on the left?” he asked his orderly.

“That’s the Anglian camp, sir.”

“Are you sure. I can’t see their colors. They must have moved their camp.”

===Chinese===
